### TL;DR

Experimental demonstration of temporally multiplexed ion-ion entanglement over 1.2 km fiber, a key step toward quantum repeaters.

### Motivation

Building large-scale quantum networks requires long-distance entanglement distribution; ion traps are promising but face challenges in extending entanglement over fiber.

### Method

Trapped ytterbium ions generate entanglement via a photonic interface; temporal multiplexing is used to enhance entanglement generation rate over 1.2 km of optical fiber.

### Result

Achieved ion-ion entanglement over 1.2 km fiber with high fidelity and rate, demonstrating temporal multiplexing as a viable technique.

### Conclusion

This work advances practical quantum repeaters by showing that temporally multiplexed ion-photon interfaces can distribute entanglement over kilometer-scale distances.

- **Tags**: `entanglement`  `Gibbs states`  `Gaussian states`  `fermionic systems`  `classical simulation`

### TL;DR

This paper proves that high-temperature Gibbs states of fermionic systems are mixtures of Gaussian states, enabling efficient classical simulation.

### Motivation

Simulating quantum Gibbs states at high temperature is important for understanding many-body physics, but generic states are hard to simulate; structural properties like Gaussianity can enable efficient algorithms.

### Method

The authors use cluster expansion techniques and properties of fermionic Gaussian states to show that the Gibbs state can be expressed as a convex combination of Gaussian states above a certain temperature threshold.

### Result

They establish a rigorous bound on the temperature above which the Gibbs state is a mixture of Gaussian states, and demonstrate that this implies efficient classical simulation via Monte Carlo methods.

### Conclusion

High-temperature fermionic Gibbs states are classically simulable, extending recent results on entanglement sudden death to fermionic systems and providing a new structural insight for quantum many-body physics.

*Keywords: Gibbs states, Gaussian states, fermionic systems, classical simulation, cluster expansion*

### TL;DR

This paper proposes a quantum sensing protocol that uses indefinite causal order (ICO) to achieve Heisenberg-limited scaling even in the presence of noise, circumventing no-go theorems.

### Motivation

Heisenberg-limited quantum sensing is fundamentally limited by noise, and standard quantum error correction imposes stringent requirements that are often impractical. The authors aim to achieve noise-resilient Heisenberg-limited sensing without these constraints.

### Method

The protocol employs a quantum switch to create an indefinite causal order of two noisy sensing channels, effectively implementing a noise-correcting operation that protects the sensing signal without active error correction.

### Result

The ICO-based protocol achieves Heisenberg-limited scaling for frequency estimation under various noise models, including dephasing and amplitude damping, with a scaling factor that matches the ideal noiseless case.

### Conclusion

Indefinite causal order provides a practical resource for noise-resilient quantum sensing, offering a new approach to overcome fundamental limitations in metrology.

*Keywords: quantum sensing, Heisenberg limit, indefinite causal order, quantum error correction, quantum metrology*

- **Tags**: `quantum battery`  `daemonic ergotropy`  `ergotropy`  `quantum thermodynamics`  `work extraction`

### TL;DR

This paper demonstrates that daemonic advantage can be harnessed to charge a quantum battery using a single auxiliary charger, bridging the gap between daemonic ergotropy and standard ergotropy.

### Motivation

Quantum batteries are promising for energy storage, but their charging efficiency is limited. The daemonic gap represents the difference between maximum extractable work with and without quantum information, and bridging this gap could enhance battery performance.

### Method

The authors consider a protocol where a quantum battery is time-evolved collectively with an auxiliary charger, then energy is extracted by tracing out the charger. They define and analyze the minimum daemonic ergotropy and compare it to the maximum standard ergotropy.

### Result

They show that daemonic advantage can be achieved, effectively bridging the daemonic gap, and that this advantage can be used to charge multimode batteries via a single auxiliary system.

### Conclusion

The work provides a practical method to enhance quantum battery charging using quantum information, with potential applications in quantum thermodynamics and energy storage.

*Keywords: quantum battery, daemonic ergotropy, ergotropy, quantum thermodynamics, work extraction*

### TL;DR

Provides a complete mathematical theory for entanglement of mixtures of Dicke states, linking separability to completely positive tensors and disproving a conjecture about PPT entanglement.

### Motivation

To understand entanglement in the important subclass of Dicke states, which arise in bosonic systems, and to bridge multipartite entanglement theory with semialgebraic geometry.

### Method

Introduces a tensor-based parametrization where diagonal entries are encoded as symmetric tensors, mapping separability to completely positive tensors, PPT to moment tensors, and entanglement witnesses to copositive tensors.

### Result

Establishes existence of PPT entanglement for all multipartite systems with local dimension ≥3 and ≥3 parties, disproving a recent conjecture; shows PPT condition for most balanced bipartition implies all others; connects bosonic extendibility to hierarchies for non-negative polynomials.

### Conclusion

Provides semidefinite programming relaxations for separability and entanglement testing in the Dicke subspace, advancing entanglement theory and its computational tools.

*Keywords: Dicke states, entanglement, completely positive tensors, PPT entanglement, semidefinite programming*

### TL;DR

This paper tightly characterizes the query complexity of quantum phase estimation with advice states, providing nearly matching upper and lower bounds for all parameter regimes.

### Motivation

Phase estimation is a fundamental subroutine in quantum computing, but its cost when given imperfect advice (states with partial overlap with the top eigenspace) was not well understood. The authors aim to close this gap.

### Method

The authors develop algorithms for phase estimation with advice and prove nearly matching lower bounds using the polynomial method with trigonometric polynomials, as well as reductions from the unitary recurrence time problem.

### Result

They show that a small number of advice copies do not significantly help, and that having many copies does not reduce cost much either. They also derive a tight bound for error probability reduction in basic phase estimation.

### Conclusion

The work provides a complete characterization of the cost of phase estimation with advice, resolving open questions and offering insights into the power of quantum advice in this context.

### TL;DR

Proposes a qudit CHSH inequality based on Wigner negativity, showing that stabilizer states maximally violate it and linking Bell violation to Wigner negativity volume.

### Motivation

Generalizing Bell inequalities to higher-dimensional systems is important for understanding nonlocality, but known results for qubits do not directly extend to qudits.

### Method

The authors introduce a new CHSH-like inequality for qudits using correlations tied to Wigner negativity of stabilizer states under a generalized π/8 gate, and analyze its violation using stabilizer states and phase-diagonal unitaries.

### Result

A specific stabilizer state achieves maximal violation among all qudit states, and the Bell operator quantifies both singlet fraction and Wigner negativity volume. Rational-phase diagonal unitaries reproduce CGLMP and SATWAP violations.

### Conclusion

Wigner negativity is necessary for nonlocality in qudit systems, and the proposed inequality provides a direct link between nonlocality and a quantum resource theory.

*Keywords: qudit CHSH inequality, Wigner negativity, nonlocality, stabilizer states, Bell violation*

### TL;DR

This paper introduces quantum walks on simplicial complexes that encode the combinatorial Laplacian, enabling superpolynomial speedups for topological data analysis and related problems.

### Motivation

Classical algorithms for topological data analysis, such as computing persistent Betti numbers, are computationally expensive. Quantum walks offer a potential speedup, but previous approaches required oracles or lacked efficient constructions for simplicial complexes.

### Method

The authors construct a quantum walk that encodes the combinatorial Laplacian by operating on a doubled state space of oriented simplices, using coherent interference to project onto harmonic cycles. They provide efficient gate complexity for clique complexes and extend the framework to estimate persistent Betti numbers, verify QMA-hard problems, and solve the high-dimensional discrete Dirichlet problem.

### Result

The quantum walk achieves an apparent superpolynomial speedup over classical algorithms, assuming an inverse-polynomially bounded spectral gap and efficient simplex sampling. The construction requires O(poly(n, 1/δ, log(1/ε))) gates.

### Conclusion

This work demonstrates a novel quantum advantage in topological data analysis without quantum oracles, opening new avenues for quantum algorithms in computational topology and complexity theory.

### TL;DR

This paper extends the Caldeira-Leggett model to non-equilibrium Gaussian reservoirs, enabling the study of quantum thermodynamics with strong coupling and squeezed/displaced modes.

### Motivation

Standard quantum thermodynamics often assumes weak coupling or equilibrium reservoirs; this work aims to model realistic non-equilibrium environments and understand thermodynamic laws in such settings.

### Method

The authors introduce a non-equilibrium Caldeira-Leggett model with squeezed and displaced thermal reservoirs, treat squeezing and displacement as generalized Hamiltonians on a modified Keldysh contour, and compute heat statistics.

### Result

They show that strongly displaced/squeezed reservoirs can generate effective time dependence and pure work, and that the fluctuation-dissipation relation is broken but reconciled with the second law. A quantum-classical correspondence for heat statistics is established, and a fluctuation theorem for energy balance is proven.

### Conclusion

The model provides a versatile framework for quantum thermodynamics far from equilibrium, with implications for work extraction, heat statistics, and thermodynamic symmetries.

### Motivation

Understanding entanglement Hamiltonians is crucial for characterizing quantum phases, but their experimental extraction is challenging. Variational methods offer a practical approach, yet their convergence properties and reliability need systematic study.

### Method

The authors interpret the cost functional as an integral, enabling iterative quadrature schemes to reduce measurements. They also introduce a modified ansatz to capture deviations from the Bisognano-Wichmann form in lattice models.

### Result

The proposed methods reduce required measurements by over an order of magnitude even with noise, improve convergence and trainability, and provide a cost-function-level diagnostic for quantum phase transitions. ### Conclusion

Variational entanglement Hamiltonians are a powerful tool for studying quantum phases, and the proposed techniques enhance their efficiency and reliability, particularly for topological phases.

### TL;DR

The symplectic rank is introduced as a unified measure of non-Gaussianity for bosonic quantum states, quantifying the minimum resources needed for preparation, compression, and measurement.

### Motivation

Non-Gaussian states are essential for quantum advantage in continuous-variable systems, but a unified measure of non-Gaussianity was lacking. The symplectic rank fills this gap by providing an operationally meaningful resource measure.

### Method

The authors define the symplectic rank based on the minimal number of Gaussian unitaries required to prepare a state from a Gaussian state, and derive its properties using symplectic geometry and quantum information theory.

### Result

The symplectic rank is shown to be a monotone under Gaussian operations, and it determines the minimal number of non-Gaussian operations needed for state preparation, the compression rate, and the number of measurements required for tomography.

### Result

They find that negativity, a signature of nonclassicality, persists for times longer than the Ehrenfest time even in the presence of moderate loss, and they characterize the three timescales governing the dynamics.

### Conclusion

### TL;DR

This paper proves impossibility results for addressing logical qubits in high-rate CSS codes using local Clifford circuits, and shows that permuting logical qubits via physical permutations is limited by code rate.

### Motivation

Recent advances in asymptotically good quantum codes raise the question of how to implement logical gates on subsets of logical qubits, which is crucial for fault-tolerant quantum computation.

### Method

The authors use automorphism theory of CSS codes and combinatorial arguments to derive no-go theorems for addressability under various circuit constraints.

### Result

They show that for CSS codes with non-zero rate, one cannot address logical X, Z, H, or S gates to a strict subset of logical qubits using 1-local Clifford circuits, and that permuting logical qubits via physical permutations is impossible if the rate exceeds 1/3 and distance is at least 3.

### Conclusion

The trade-off between addressability and code efficiency is fundamental, and this work provides new insights for designing fault-tolerant quantum computations with high-rate codes.

*Keywords: CSS codes, addressability, logical gates, fault tolerance, automorphisms*

### Method

The authors reduce unitary implementation to Grover search, constructing circuits with linear depth and ancillae, and prove matching lower bounds for a class of implementations.

### Result

Any n-qubit unitary can be implemented approximately in time O(2^{n/2}) with query access to a classical oracle, and exactly by a circuit of depth O(2^{n/2}) with one- and two-qubit gates and O(2^{n/2}) ancillae.

### Motivation

Simulating quantum systems with temporally correlated classical noise (e.g., 1/f noise) is computationally challenging; existing methods require fine time grids.

### Method

The noise is decomposed into a sum of Ornstein-Uhlenbeck processes, then conditioned on a coarse realization; the deterministic part captures the coarse behavior while bridge processes are averaged analytically.

The method yields a simple concatenation rule and allows precomputation of noise propagators, demonstrated with numerical examples.

### Conclusion

Temporal coarse-graining efficiently handles multi-scale classical noise in quantum simulations, with practical advantages for solid-state quantum processors.

### Motivation

Magic state cultivation is a key technique for fault-tolerant quantum computing, but simulating the non-Clifford components is computationally expensive. The authors aim to reduce simulation cost by exploiting structure in the circuits.

### Method

The authors develop stabiliser decompositions that represent the non-Clifford state as a sum of Clifford ZX-diagrams, using cutting techniques to reduce the number of terms. They also provide numerical simulations with optimisations.

### Result

They achieve more than a 10-fold reduction in the number of terms compared to a naive stabiliser decomposition, and demonstrate nearly 1000 shots per second on a laptop for small circuits at uniform noise.

### TL;DR

### Motivation

Linear optics without post-selection cannot produce arbitrary quantum states, limiting applications that require entangled resources. Understanding the constraints of linear optical state preparation is essential.

### Method

The authors identify conserved quantities (invariants) under the action of passive linear optical Hamiltonians by mapping the density matrix spectrum onto the Lie algebra. They derive a lower bound on the distance between output and target states based on invariant differences.

### Result

The invariants provide necessary conditions for exact state preparation and a lower bound for approximate preparation, enabling the exclusion of impossible evolutions.

### Conclusion

Future state preparation methods must consider these invariants to avoid impossible linear optical transformations, narrowing the search for useful entangled states like NOON states.

A machine learning decoder for bivariate bicycle codes achieves lower logical error rates and faster runtime than BP-OSD on small codes, but performance degrades on larger codes.

### Motivation

Fault-tolerant quantum computing requires efficient decoders for quantum LDPC codes, but existing decoders like BP-OSD have variable runtime and limited performance on small codes.

### Method

A recurrent, transformer-based neural network is trained to decode circuit-level noise on bivariate bicycle codes, using syndrome measurements as input.

### Result

On the [144,12,12] BB code, the ML decoder achieves logical error rates almost 10 times lower than BP-OSD and is an order of magnitude faster, but on the [288,12,18] code it yields worse logical error rates.

### Conclusion

ML decoders can outperform conventional decoders on small QLDPC codes, but scaling to larger codes requires more complex architectures or training procedures.

*Keywords: quantum LDPC codes, machine learning decoding, bivariate bicycle codes, fault tolerance, neural network decoder*

To rigorously establish a sufficient criterion for quantum integrability, clarifying the boundary between integrable and nonintegrable many-body systems.

### Result

### Conclusion

### TL;DR

This paper studies rectified transport of active Brownian particles in asymmetric ratchets by adjusting internal parameters like mass and center of mass, revealing a 'parameters readjustment effect' and anomalous reentrant phase transitions.

### Motivation

Most existing work on active ratchets focuses on structural boundaries; this work shifts to internal properties of active particles to understand their influence on transport.

### Method

Experiments using a vibration-driven vehicle as a macroscopic model for active Brownian particles, combined with coarse-grained numerical simulations, systematically vary mass and center of mass position.

### Result

Increasing mass reduces average velocity and persistence length, leading to a noise-dominated localized state; shifting center of mass induces translational-rotational coupling causing non-monotonic reentrant phase transitions in rectification probability.

### Conclusion

Internal parameter evolution is a core determinant of macroscopic transport behavior, offering principles for designing efficient active rectification systems beyond geometric constraints.

*Keywords: active Brownian particles, asymmetric ratchets, rectified transport, translational-rotational coupling, reentrant phase transition*

### Motivation

To overcome the reversible energy exchange that limits net acceleration in direct laser acceleration (DLA) schemes.

### Method

A test-electron model with prescribed fields is used to show that a slow longitudinal increase of the quasi-static plasma magnetic field introduces hysteresis in the betatron-to-laser frequency ratio.

The hysteresis enables phase control of the electron-laser energy exchange and suppresses reversibility, allowing sustained energy gain.

### Conclusion

Longitudinal variation of the plasma magnetic field can qualitatively change DLA dynamics, offering a new control knob for laser-driven particle acceleration.

### Motivation

Existing network reconstruction methods focus on binary interactions and dynamics-based data, but real-world data often involve coarse-grained interaction states and multi-body interactions.

### Method

The method formulates an optimization model based on the minimum description length principle, uses MCMC sampling to generate candidate configurations, constructs a Bayesian network guided by minimum-entropy, and iteratively updates via distribution estimation.

### Result

Experiments on synthetic and real-world datasets demonstrate the effectiveness and robustness of the proposed method for inferring higher-order network structures.

### Conclusion

The proposed Bayesian network reconstruction method successfully infers higher-order network structures from low-resolution interaction state data.

### TL;DR

This paper studies photoionization delays in attosecond pump-probe experiments, focusing on the breakdown of the separability of Wigner and Coulomb-laser-coupling delays at low photoelectron energies.

### Motivation

Interpreting time delays at low photoelectron energies or with short IR wavelengths is crucial for ultrafast experiments in atoms and molecules, but no consensus exists on how to separate delay components.

### Method

The authors derive an analytical cut-off law that defines the minimum energy where the total delay can be approximated as the sum of Wigner and CLC components, and apply it to various probe laser frequencies.

### Result

The breakdown between short-range and long-range delay components begins around 23 eV for IR wavelengths near 400 nm, which is higher than traditionally assumed.

### Conclusion

The findings have important implications for interpreting photoionization delay experiments probed with visible light, especially in the low-energy regime.

*Keywords: photoionization delays, attosecond physics, Wigner delay, Coulomb-laser-coupling delay, ultrafast dynamics*

- **Tags**: `noise`  `plasma`  `dynamics`  `lasing`  `ionization`

### TL;DR

This paper proposes a deterministic seeding mechanism for inner-shell x-ray lasers using two-color Raman driving, shifting the lasing onset from stochastic noise to a driven coherence.

### Motivation

Conventional inner-shell x-ray lasers are initiated by amplified spontaneous emission (ASE), leading to stochastic temporal onset and phase. The authors aim to achieve deterministic control over the lasing process.

### Method

The authors analyze a regime where a resonant two-color Raman drive induces a core-valence coherence at the lasing frequency, acting as a seed. They use a microscopic density-matrix description coupled to a linear Maxwell-Bloch-Langevin propagation model with calibrated spontaneous-emission noise, and derive an analytical criterion separating noise-dominated from coherence-dominated regimes.

### Result

An explicit analytical criterion is derived, depending on Raman drive amplitude, small-signal gain, gate duration, and noise strength. Numerical integration confirms the predicted crossover. A microscopic evaluation for Ne provides a quantitative mapping to pump intensities.

### Conclusion

The mechanism enables a coherence-dominated operating regime for inner-shell x-ray lasing, where the amplified field originates from a driven microscopic coherence rather than spontaneous fluctuations, offering deterministic control over the laser initiation.

*Keywords: inner-shell x-ray laser, Raman driving, coherence seeding, amplified spontaneous emission, Maxwell-Bloch-Langevin model*

### TL;DR

This paper studies the relationship between national energy intensity and global decarbonization, finding that offshoring of energy-intensive production creates an illusion of decarbonization.

### Motivation

Declining national energy intensity of GDP is often interpreted as progress in decarbonization, but this may be misleading if energy-intensive production is simply moved abroad.

### Method

The authors analyze global supply chains and energy intensity data to show that reductions in national energy intensity can be offset by increased energy use in other countries due to offshoring.

### Result

The study demonstrates that accounting for global supply chains reveals that apparent decarbonization at the national level does not necessarily imply global decarbonization.

### Conclusion

Policymakers should consider global supply chain effects when assessing decarbonization progress, as national metrics can be deceptive.

*Keywords: energy intensity, offshoring, decarbonization, global supply chains*

- **Source**: Rev. Mod. Phys.  |  **Feed**: Reviews of Modern Physics

- **Tags**: `anisotropy`  `cosmic dipole`  `cosmological principle`  `ΛCDM`  `large-scale structure`

### TL;DR

This paper reviews the cosmic dipole anomaly, a potential challenge to the cosmological principle and ΛCDM model, based on large-scale structure surveys.

### Motivation

To test the cosmological principle by comparing the dipole anisotropy in the matter distribution with the CMB dipole, which could indicate a violation of the standard model of cosmology.

### Method

The authors analyze recent catalogs of radio galaxies and quasars, comparing the observed dipole in their distribution with the CMB dipole, and review potential biases and systematic effects.

### Result

A significant inconsistency is found between the matter dipole and the CMB dipole, suggesting a possible violation of the cosmological principle.

### Conclusion

The cosmic dipole anomaly may challenge the ΛCDM model, but further investigation is needed to rule out systematic biases.

*Keywords: cosmic dipole, cosmological principle, ΛCDM, large-scale structure, CMB*

- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1367-2630/ae6df0](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1367-2630/ae6df0)
- **Tags**: `plasma`  `topologically-protected surface plasma waves`  `collisionless damping`  `non-uniform magnetized plasma`  `upper-hybrid resonance`

### TL;DR

This paper studies topologically-protected surface plasma waves in non-uniformly magnetized plasma columns, focusing on wave propagation and collisionless damping.

### Motivation

To extend the theory of topologically-protected surface plasma waves from uniformly magnetized to realistic three-dimensional magnetic fields, and to understand wave absorption in non-uniform plasmas.

### Method

Theoretical analysis of wave propagation in a cold plasma column with a non-uniform magnetic field produced by finite-sized coils, including collisionless damping via upper-hybrid resonances.

### Result

Surface plasma waves are collisionlessly absorbed in a localized evanescent region as they propagate into decreasing magnetic field, with negligible reflection compared to unmagnetized plasmas.

### Conclusion

The findings demonstrate robust, reflection-free absorption of topologically-protected surface waves in realistic non-uniform magnetic fields, relevant to plasma applications.

*Keywords: topologically-protected surface plasma waves, collisionless damping, non-uniform magnetized plasma, upper-hybrid resonance*

## 489. Orthogonal Phase Modulation And Lissajous Mode Decoupling In Light-induced Thermoelastic Spectroscopy For Real-time Multi-component Gas Sensing

- **Score**: 0.0 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: Rep. Prog. Phys.  |  **Feed**: Reports on Progress in Physics (ROPP)
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-06-18
- **Authors**: Hanxu Ma, Shunda Qiao, Ying He, Haiyue Sun And Yufei Ma
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae79a3](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae79a3)
- **Tags**: `noise`  `spectroscopy`  `optimization`  `real-time`  `orthogonal phase modulation`

### TL;DR

This paper presents a novel optical gas sensing technique using orthogonal phase modulation and Lissajous mode decoupling in light-induced thermoelastic spectroscopy for real-time multi-component gas detection.

### Motivation

Real-time multi-component gas sensing is important for environmental monitoring and industrial safety, but existing methods suffer from channel crosstalk and complexity.

### Method

The authors use two continuous-wave lasers modulated at the same frequency with a 45° phase difference, combined via wavelength division multiplexing to excite a quartz tuning fork. The piezoelectric signal is orthogonally demodulated to decouple the two gas concentration signals.

### Result

The sensor achieves linear responses for methane and acetylene with low systematic errors (0.54% and 0.79%) and detection limits of 0.32 ppm and 0.29 ppm, respectively.

### Conclusion

The proposed OPM-LITES sensor provides a simple and efficient solution for real-time multi-component gas detection with a novel theoretical framework for mode decoupling.

*Keywords: orthogonal phase modulation, light-induced thermoelastic spectroscopy, quartz tuning fork, multi-component gas sensing, Lissajous mode decoupling*

- **Tags**: `disorder`  `NLRP3 inflammasome`  `centrosome`  `inflammation`  `protein assembly`

### TL;DR

This paper investigates the spatial organization of NLRP3 inflammasome assembly, focusing on the centrosome as a hub for regulatory factors.

### Motivation

The NLRP3 inflammasome is a key mediator of inflammation in various diseases, but the spatial principles governing its assembly are poorly understood.

### Method

The study uses cell biology and biochemical approaches to identify and characterize the role of the centrosome in recruiting and organizing NLRP3 inflammasome components.

### Result

The centrosome acts as a central hub that unifies regulatory factors required for NLRP3 inflammasome activation.

### Conclusion

Understanding the spatial organization of inflammasome assembly may provide new targets for therapeutic intervention in inflammatory diseases.

*Keywords: NLRP3 inflammasome, centrosome, inflammation, protein assembly*

### TL;DR

This paper uses large language models to identify decision reasons from verbal reports, achieving high alignment with actual choices.

### Motivation

Understanding why people make choices is central to decision science, but extracting reasons from free-text reports is challenging.

### Method

The authors apply large language models to analyze free-text reports and predict the choices implied by the stated reasons.

The models achieve 95% alignment between actual choices and those implied by the identified reasons.

Chuahttps://ror.org/02jz4aj89department Of Advanced Computing Sciences, Maastricht University, Maastricht 6229 EN, The Netherlandsbhttps://ror.org/00za53h95department Of Neurology, Johns Hopkins University, Baltimore, MD 21205chttps://ror.org/05q6tgt32department Of Neurology, Kennedy Krieger Institute, Baltimore, MD 21205dhttps://ror.org/002pd6e78department Of Neurosurgery, Massachusetts General Hospital, Boston, MA 02114eharvard Medical School, Boston, MA 02215fhttps://ror.org/002pd6e78department Of Neurology, Massachusetts General Hospital, Boston, MA 02114ghttps://ror.org/05qwgg493department Of Mathematics And Statistics, Boston University, Boston, MA 02215hhttps://ror.org/05qwgg493center For Systems Neuroscience, Department Of Mathematics And Statistics, Boston University, Boston, MA 02215
- **Link**: [https://www.pnas.org/doi/abs/10.1073/pnas.2517454123?af=R](https://www.pnas.org/doi/abs/10.1073/pnas.2517454123?af=R)
- **Tags**: `memory`  `oscillations`  `sleep rhythms`  `memory consolidation`  `epileptic spikes`

### TL;DR

This paper studies sleep-dependent memory consolidation and its disruption by epileptic spikes in human epilepsy, focusing on neural oscillations.

### Motivation

To understand how sleep rhythms (slow oscillations, spindles, ripples) coordinate motor memory consolidation and how epileptic activity interferes.

### Method

Simultaneous intracranial EEG recordings from cortex, thalamus, and hippocampus in human epilepsy patients during sleep, analyzing oscillatory coupling and spike effects.

### Result

A hierarchical cascade of sleep rhythms supports motor memory, and epileptic spikes disrupt this cascade, impairing consolidation.

### Conclusion

Sleep rhythm coordination is crucial for memory, and epilepsy-related spikes can hijack this process, offering a target for therapeutic intervention.

*Keywords: sleep rhythms, memory consolidation, epileptic spikes, intracranial EEG, motor memory*
